115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

寄与度を示す。「みずほGDPナウ」は各経済指標の結果を公表
前に予測しており （モデル予測値） 、 寄与度はモデル予測値と
実績値の乖離幅から計算される。 なお、 各経済指標の確報を受

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

［参考文献］ 
浦沢聡士 （2023）「GDPナウキャストと景気判断～景気判断実務におけるGDPナウキャストの活用に向
けて～」 、内閣府経済社会総合研究所「経済分析」第208号 
太田晴康・仲山泰弘・酒井才介・松浦大将・越山祐資・西野洋平（2024） 「 「みずほGDPナウ」の推計
～DFMを用いた日本のGDPナウキャスティング～」 、 みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ 『みずほ
インサイト』 、2024年8月30日 
酒井才介・西野洋平・太田晴康・仲山泰弘（2024）「「みずほ GDP ナウ」で見る景気動向～9 月中旬
時点で 7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.0％と推計～」、みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ
『Mizuho RT EXPRESS』、2024 年 9 月 19 日 
服部直樹（2026）「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期 2 次 QE）」、みずほ総合
研究所『QE 解説』、2026 年 9 月 8 日

### Page 4

4

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

美元兌日圓匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額 7,739 億美元，月增 1.2%、年增 6.0%，
扭轉前月下滑走勢；核心零售(扣除汽車、零件及加油站)月增 1.2%。其
中非實體零售商、加油站及電子家電商店銷售成長，顯示美國消費需求
仍具支撐力道。 
⚫ 8 月美國住宅市場表現分化，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4 萬戶，月減
2.7%；新屋開工降至 127.5 萬戶，月減2.6%，惟單戶住宅開工升至 91.8
萬戶 、 創近5 個月新高 。 另一方面 ， 在建住宅年減3.2%、 完工年減27.1%，
顯示高利率與需求不確定性仍抑制整體住宅建設活動。 
⚫ 9 月聯邦公開市場委員會(FOMC)決議以 12 比 0 一致升息 1 碼至 3.75%
至 4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。委員會認為美國經濟及勞動
市場仍具韌性，惟通膨持續高於 2%目標，因此採取升息行動並重申致
力維持價格穩定，政策立場轉趨鷹派；同時刪除前瞻指引措辭，強調未
來政策將依據經濟數據及展望調整。最新經濟預測(SEP)顯示，2026 年
GDP 成長率上修至 2.3%、失業率下修至 4.1%，PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨
率則分別上修至 3.7%及 3.4%，利率中位數升至 4.1%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 在 9 月 24 日美國華府「川習會」前，美國財長貝森特與中國副總理何
立峰於紐約會談，聚焦AI、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦產供應等議題。市
場預期雙方將以維持對話、避免貿易緊張升級為主，短期內難有重大突
破。 
⚫ 美國在 USMCA 談判中要求墨西哥提高 AI 伺服器及半導體產品的北美
原產地比重，並限制中國等非北美零組件使用，以防堵企業藉由墨西哥
組裝規避美國關稅。由於墨西哥已成為美國 AI 伺服器重要供應基地，
若新規定上路 ， 恐促使包括台廠在內的供應鏈提高北美在地採購與生產
比重，進一步重塑北美 AI 硬體供應鏈布局。 
⚫ 美國總統川普簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與伊朗法
案》 ，強化對俄、伊制裁，並授權對大量採購俄羅斯能源國家商品課徵
最高 100%關稅。

稅共識，將有助降低全球經貿不確定性並穩定市場信心。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.21 
一、 美國—消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
2026 年 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,739 億美元，較
上月增加 1.2%，優於 7 月修正後月減 0.5%的表現，年增率亦達 6.0%，顯示美國
消費支出動能於 8 月明顯回升。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增1.4%、年增
6.9%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增1.2%、年增 5.6%，
反映核心消費需求維持穩健成長。從主要業別表現觀察，非實體零售商銷售額月
增 2.6%、加油站月增 3.1%、電子及家電商店月增 1.6%，食品服務及飲酒場所亦
月增 1.2%，顯示線上消費、能源相關支出及服務性消費持續成長；另一方面，建
築材料與園藝用品經銷商月減 0.2%，對整體零售表現形成部分抵銷。整體而言，
8 月美國零售銷售由前月下滑轉為明顯成長，且扣除汽車及能源相關項目後仍維
持穩健增幅，顯示美國消費需求仍具支撐力道，對經濟活動形成正面挹注，惟後
續仍須持續關注物價變動、金融情勢及消費信心對零售支出的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國住宅市場持續呈現分化走勢，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4
萬戶，月減 2.7%，惟較去年同期增加 3.5%；其中單戶住宅建築許可 87.8 萬戶，
月減 1.8%，5 戶以上多戶住宅建築許可 46.7 萬戶，月減 3.1%，顯示住宅開發規
劃動能較前月略有降溫 。 另一方面 ， 新屋開工年化數降至127.5 萬戶 ， 月減2.6%、
年減 1.2%，其中單戶住宅開工回升至 91.8 萬戶，月增 7.6%、年增 5.2%，為近 5
個月最高水準 ； 惟5 戶以上多戶住宅開工降至 34.4 萬戶 ， 月減22.5%、 年減15.5%，
拖累整體開工表現。施工方面，在建住宅年化數為 127.1 萬戶，月增 0.3%，但較
去年同期減少 3.2%，顯示住宅建設規模仍呈收斂趨勢；完工年化數則降至 112.8
萬戶，月減 11.9%、年減 27.1%，反映住宅供給落成速度明顯放緩。整體而言，8
月美國住宅市場呈現單戶住宅需求相對具支撐、但多戶住宅及整體營建活動偏弱
的格局，顯示在高利率與需求前景仍具不確定性的環境下，建商投資態度仍趨審
慎，後續仍須關注利率走勢及房市需求變化對住宅市場的影響。 
2026 年 9 月聯邦公開市場委員會(FOMC)會議決議以 12 比 0 一致同意將聯邦
基金利率目標區間調升 1 碼至 3.75%至 4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息，謹
將本次會議聲明 、 最新經濟預測摘要(SEP)及主席 Kevin Warsh 記者會重點摘錄如

下：(一)儘管地緣政治風險推升經濟前景不確定性，美國經濟活動仍維持穩健擴
張，國內支出展現韌性，生產力成長與資本投資維持強勁，勞動市場表現穩定，
就業增幅大致與勞動力成長相符，失業率變動不大；(二)通膨仍高於聯準會 2%目
標，委員會認為近期數據尚未充分顯示通膨正以足夠速度回落至目標水準，因此
決議升息 1 碼，以支持通膨更及時回歸 2%目標，並重申將致力實現價格穩定；
(三)本次會議在政策溝通上出現調整，聲明刪除過去偏向寬鬆的前瞻指引措辭並
簡化內容；主席 Kevin Warsh 於記者會中明確表示不提供前瞻指引，亦不預判未
來會議結果 ， 強調後續政策行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及整體經濟展望決定 ；(四)
最新 SEP 顯示，2026 年實質 GDP 成長率預測中位數由 2.2%上修至 2.3%，失業
率由 4.3%下修至 4.1%；PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率則分別上修至 3.7%及 3.4%，顯
示通膨壓力仍具黏性 。 利率方面 ，點陣圖顯示 2026 年底聯邦基金利率預測由 3.8%
升至 4.1%，2027 年底維持 4.1%，官員仍保留進一步升息可能，整體政策立場較
6 月轉趨鷹派；(五)主席 Kevin Warsh 表示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，通膨
仍高於目標且地緣政治風險升溫 ， 為本次升息主因 。 未來聯準會不提供前瞻指引 ，
也不預設利率路徑，將依經濟數據與展望機動調整政策。 
在 9 月 24 日美國華府白宮舉行之 「川習會」 前 ， 美國財長貝森特(Scott Bessent)
與中國國務院副總理何立峰於紐約摩根大通總部舉行會談，美國貿易代表葛里爾
(Jamieson Greer) 亦共同參與。雙方將就人工智慧 (AI)、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦
產供應等議題交換意見，並為川普與習近平會晤預作準備。討論重點包括將於11
月 10 日到期之美中貿易休戰安排、中國稀土磁材及關鍵礦產出口供應情況，以
及 AI 模型安全與治理機制等議題。此外，雙方亦將檢視今年 5 月北京會談後尚
待落實之事項，包括降低部分非戰略性商品關稅、中國增加美國農產品採購及波
音客機採購計畫等。市場普遍預期本次會談將以維持既有合作架構及避免貿易緊
張升級為主要目標，短期內出現重大突破的可能性仍相對有限，美中關稅、關鍵
礦產供應及 AI 治理議題預料將成為本次川習會的重要焦點。 
美國政府近期在美墨加協定(USMCA)檢討談判中，要求墨西哥針對人工智慧
(AI)伺服器、半導體及相關硬體產品建立更嚴格的原產地規範，擬限制來自中國
等非北美地區零組件占比，並可能參照汽車產業的區域含量標準，以防堵外國企
業透過墨西哥組裝後出口美國，規避美國對中國商品實施的關稅與貿易限制。市
場分析指出，在美國 AI 基礎建設需求帶動下，墨西哥已成為重要 AI 伺服器生產

6

基地，今年上半年 AI 資料中心伺服器出口金額達 830 億美元，其中 94%銷往美
國，並超越汽車成為對美最大出口項目。由於鴻海等台灣電子代工廠在墨西哥設
有 AI 伺服器生產據點，若相關規範正式上路，供應鏈可能被迫提高北美在地採
購及生產比重，進一步重塑北美 AI 硬體供應鏈布局。美墨雙方預計 9 月底於華
府展開新一輪談判，惟相關原產地門檻、過渡期間及適用範圍尚未定案。 
美國總統川普已於 9 月 18 日簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與
伊朗法案》，該法案此前分別以參議院 86 票對 11 票、眾議院 262 票對 159 票通
過。法案除擴大對俄羅斯政府官員、金融機構及協助能源運輸的「影子艦隊」制
裁外，亦授權總統對持續大量購買俄羅斯石油及天然氣的第三國商品課徵最高
100%關稅，其中中國、印度等俄羅斯能源主要買家可能受到影響。法案目的在於
削弱俄羅斯能源出口收入及其支撐烏俄戰爭的財政來源，並強化對伊朗的相關制
裁。 
二、歐元區—美歐矛盾與歐中貿易協商在即，增添工業復甦變數 
 歐元區 7 月工業生產較 6 月下滑 0.1%，為連續兩個月衰退，其中非耐久消費
財產出減少 1.6%，抵銷中間財、資本財、消費耐久財分別成長0.3%、0.5%、0.9%
的影響，主要是美伊衝突帶來的庫存儲備需求持續消退，抵銷德國軍備採購及消
暑用品消費增加的影響。以國別而言，德國工業生產下跌1.5%最為疲軟，且為該
國近一年最大跌幅，主要是部分汽車大廠決定提前進行歲修，故預期待歲修結束
後，8、9 月份可望出現汽車庫存回補的生產需求，此外，在德國軍備採購需求的
支持下 ， 德國7 月工業訂單仍大幅成長 2.5%， 相關產出可望在第三季末及第四季
陸續反映；法、義、西工業生產月增率則分別為-0.4%、0.7%、0.6%。 
 整體而言，美伊衝突爆發後，能源密集產業普遍增產，但該類產業從 6 月起
已有較明顯的趨緩跡象，其他主要產業則大致呈現橫向波動，顯示工業生產趨勢
仍屬溫和。惟近期歐盟提議讓加拿大成為歐盟準會員國，引發美國不滿，可能進
一步升高美歐貿易與外交摩擦；此外，歐盟與中國預定於 10 月 8 日針對貿易失
衡問題進行重要協商 ， 後續若涉及貿易措施調整 ， 亦可能影響歐元區製造業表現 。 
三、日本—央行升息 1 碼，12 月仍有再升息空間 
7 月核心機械訂單月減 3.7%，其中製造業訂單小幅下滑，主要受到化學
產業訂單減少影響，惟電子產品及電氣機械訂單持續成長，反映人工智慧相

關產業及供應鏈企業仍積極擴充產能，支撐資本支出動能。 雖然非製造業訂
單則因資通訊及資訊服務業需求回落影響而表現較弱，但在人力短缺與數位
轉型需求支撐下，投資動能仍具韌性。綜合來看，企業獲利穩健及AI 投資熱
潮延續，預期日本企業資本支出仍將維持成長態勢。 
8 月出口年增 19.3%，半導體相關產品仍為主要成長動能，其中半導體等
電子零件出口大幅成長 52.3%。從地區來看，對美國、中國及歐盟出口分別
成長 24.9%、20.6%及 11.0%，半導體等電子零組件對中國出口金額，以及對
美國 、 歐盟的半導體製造設備出口均較去年同期倍增，顯示全球 AI 及高科技
產業投資需求持續支撐日本出口表現。 
8 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 1.9%，主要受到政府恢復電力與天然
氣補貼，以及米價回落抑制食品價格所致。從細項來看，石油產品價格上漲
帶動家庭消費品等核心商品價格續漲；服務類通膨除通訊費用趨穩外，其他
服務項目已開始出現漲價跡象。展望未來，能源成本上升及企業將 薪資成本
逐步轉嫁至售價，預料將推動通膨壓力由商品擴散至更多服務領域，使通膨
維持緩步升溫趨勢，並進一步支持央行持續推動貨幣政策正常化。 
日本央行於 9 月 17、18 日召開貨幣政策會議，宣布將政策利率調升 1 碼
至 1.25%。雖然政策聲明整體基調變化不大，但仍持續強調通膨上行風險，
並指出目前金融條件仍相對寬鬆。 值得關注的是 ， 本次決策出現兩張反對票 ，
顯示委員會內部對經濟與通膨前景看法分歧加劇，一派認為當前經濟與通膨
動能尚不足以支持升息，另一派則認為通膨升溫風險高於預期。在會後記者
會上，總裁植田和男指出，日本貨幣政策已進入新的階段，由過去較重視通
膨低於目標的風險，轉為兼顧通膨上、下行風險，並強調未來升息步調將視
經濟與物價發展而定，不會預先承諾特定路徑。此外，他也表示 主要國家央
行升息對日本匯率及金融環境具有重要影響，因此將持續納入政策考量。雖
然總裁未透露最終利率水準，但相關談話暗示目前 1.25%的政策利率可能尚
未達到中性水準，日本央行亦未排除未來進一步升息的可能性。整體而言，
日本央行對通膨前景看法趨於樂觀，政策立場仍偏向緊縮，預期若未來數季
通膨持續走揚，日本央行仍有望於 12 月再度升息。

四、中國—8 月內需持續疲乏，與歐美的關係將為近期關注重點 
在內需方面，1-8 月累計零售額年增率較 2025 年同期增加 1.1%，較 1-7 月累
計的 1.2%持續下降。其中 8 月零售額年增率放緩 0.2 個百分點至 0.4%，反映以
舊換新的補貼力道不如過去兩年水準，加上房市低迷，以及對景氣波動較為敏感
的消費耐久財需求進一步下滑 ，8 月家具 、 汽車 、 裝潢消費分別年減7.9%、18.5%、
11.8%。另外，1-8 月固定資產投資年減幅由 6.7%擴大至 7.2%，反映美伊衝突、
政府主導去化過剩產能、美國貿易戰等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎縮產
業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等。同期間房地產開發
投資額年減幅由 19.2%擴大至 19.9%、施工面積年減幅則擴大為 13.2%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動 ， 抵銷內需疲軟的影響 ，8 月工業生產年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 5.2%(1-8 月工業生產年增率持平 1-7 月水準為 5.3%)。依
產業別來看，8 月份高科技產品仍是主要成長動能，包含 IC(成長 20.6%)、工業
機器人(34.6%)、服務機器人(5%)、 新能源車(21.9%)等 ； 惟受到原油供應減少以及
能源價格上漲壓力影響，化纖、水泥、玻璃、鋼材等產量持續衰退，另因 3C 關
鍵物料漲價影響，手機產量衰退幅度高達 22.3%，太陽能電池則因取消出口退稅
政策跌幅高達 12.9%。展望未來，若9 月 24 日川習會有正向發展，美中關稅休戰
得以延長 ， 且歐中貿易摩擦未進一步惡化 ， 將有助於穩定中國出口與製造業景氣 ，
抵銷內需疲軟的經濟壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨持穩，AI 需求持續帶動新、馬出口表現 
印度:受美伊衝突延宕與不利的氣候條件影響，8 月批發物價指數(WPI)年增率
揚升至 9.92%，其中，燃料價格飆漲22.9%，食品價格漲幅達 5.7%，另受化學
品、菸草、塑橡膠、基本金屬漲幅均逾一成影響，製造業通膨加速上揚8.4%。
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 4.82%，為 2024 年 12 月以來最高，食品
飲料（5.95%）、交通運輸（4.6%）餐飲和住宿服務（8.38%）及個人照護/社
會安全 （15.17%） 價格亦大幅上漲。儘管通膨水準仍維持在印度央行目標區間
內，惟生產成本居高不下，恐進一步外溢到消費端，並衝擊內需表現。

月以來最強勁的成長速度 ， 反映強勁的人工智慧相關需求 ， 帶動電子產品出口

MYR281 億元、年增 77.1%。 
五、台灣—央行維持利率不變並適度鬆綁不動產信用管制措施 
台灣央行於 9 月 17 日召開理監事會議，決議維持政策利率(重貼現率)於 2%
不變，央行指出，雖然國際油價維持高檔、服務類通膨仍具黏性，但國內通膨仍
屬可控範圍，2026 年 1 至 8 月通膨年增 1.84%，預估 2027 年可回落至 2%以下；
同時考量中東地緣政治、美國經貿政策及 AI 產業發展等不確定因素，因此採取
審慎觀望態度，暫不升息。 
此外，央行認為先前實施之選擇性信用管制已逐漸見效，房市交易降溫、不
動產貸款集中度下降，因此適度調整相關措施，包括將自然人第二戶購屋貸款最
高成數由 6 成提高至 7 成，並刪除購地貸款須切結於一定期間內動工興建之規
定，改由金融機構依授信實務審慎評估辦理。整體而言，本次政策方向呈現利率
維持審慎偏緊基調、房市管制微幅鬆綁。

### Page 3

Hon Hai (2317.TW; Buy, on CL): New AI server racks and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26E

We expect Hon Hai’s monthly revenues to continue sequential growth through Dec 
2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +39% QoQ, driven by: (1) AI data center: The 
next-generation AI server racks and CPO switches ramp-up, with higher ASP and early 
delivery. We expect Hon Hai to be the first to deliver the latest model, around 2-3 
months ahead of peers, driving its Oct and onward revenue growth. Followed by 
GPU-powered AI server racks, the LPU-powered AI server racks should fuel the growth 
in Dec and onward, supporting the YoY increase in 1Q27. As our Global Server TAM 
update shows, we expect the AI server rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 
2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the global server value TAM by 2028E. And (2) 
Smartphone: riding on the major brand customer’s latest model launches along with the 
new form factor to attract more end users. As we highlighted in September (report link), 
we reduced our global smartphone volume estimates by 1m/ 8m/ 0.2m units to 1.1bn / 
1.2bn/ 1.2bn in 2026-28E, or -10% / +2%/ +2% YoY on high memory costs; however, we 
remain positive on the potential for global leading brands to outperform the market 
given their strong brand awareness, large procurement power, and customers’ stronger 
consumption power. We remain positive on foldable phones and expect premium 
models to continue to gain market share, which should be positive to Hon Hai. Maintain 
Buy (on CL).

3-month revenues preview: We expect the company’s 4Q26E revenues to be +61% 
YoY/ +39% QoQ to NT$4.2 trillion. 3Q26 delivered strong revenues YoY growth of 47%, 
or 5% / 7% ahead of our / Bloomberg consensus, and we expect to see both YoY and 
MoM growth throughout Oct-Dec, driven by new AI server racks, new smartphone 
models, and CPO switches. Management noted that AI-related products remain on track 
to maintain solid growth momentum through 4Q26, while ICT products continue to 
benefit from typical 2H seasonal tailwinds, which should support overall operational 
performance in line with current consensus expectations.

### Page 4

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design, 
software, and semis; (3) slower-than-expected ramp-up of capacity globally; and (4) 
fiercer-than-expected competition in the consumer electronics EMS business.  
Quanta (2382.TW, Neutral): AI servers ramp-up to drive growth ahead; model transition and PC 
shipment slowdown could weigh on 4Q26

Quanta Sep revenues declined by 23% MoM, which we attributed to AI servers model 
transition, while PC entered quarter-end at a strong MoM of +62% MoM. Looking 
forward, we expect monthly revenues to resume sequential growth in Oct to Dec, riding 
on a reducing impact of AI servers model transition as new racks ramp up, along with 
growing general servers on agentic AI, and ASP increase on better specification and high 
raw material costs. We expect 4Q26E revenues to decline by 4% QoQ, considering the 
high base in 3Q26 and the gradual ramp-up of new AI server racks. As we highlighted in 
Oct (Global Server TAM updates), we expect global AI server racks (powered by NVIDIA 
and AMD) shipment at 63k / 110k / 164k in 2026E-28E along with the specification 
upgrade to drive the ASP; we raised leading US CSPs aggregate capex by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026E-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E, reflecting our positive view on AI spending.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$300 is based on a 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. Key 
upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers. 
Wiwynn (6669.TW, Buy): New ASIC and GPU AI server racks in expansion

Wiwynn delivered strong Sep revenues, which were 19% ahead of our estimates, 
reflecting strong general server demand from US leading CSPs, and its major customers’ 
new ASIC AI server ramping up. We expect revenue YoY growth to remain strong at mid 
double-digit through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +60 YoY / +8% QoQ, 
driven by (1) major customers’ new ASIC AI servers ramping up, across air-cooled and 
liquid-cooled, (2) general servers end demand remaining solid on agentic AI, (3) new 
GPU-powered AI server racks entering mass production in 4Q26 across NVIDIA and 
AMD platforms, and (4) potential new customers in ASIC AI servers. As we highlighted in 
Oct (Global Server TAM update), we raised AI servers implied AI chips shipment by 9% / 
19% in 2027 / 28E with ASIC penetration rate increasing to 58% by 2028E, and our US 
internet team forecasts that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will 
grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our US CSP capex 
estimates raised by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E, supporting 
our positive view on Wiwynn. Maintain Buy with an unchanged 12m TP of NT$4,000

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Wiwynn 
We are Buy-rated on Wiwynn with a 12-month TP of NT$4,000. Our target price is 
based on 20.3x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. 
Key downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected AI server demand growth; 2) 
slower-than-expected recovery in general server demand; and 3) more severe 
competition in the server ODM market.  
Wistron (3231.TW, Buy): AI server racks to see strong ramp-up with customer expansion and 
specification upgrades

Wistron’s Sep revenue was up 87% YoY to NT$381bn, or 18% lower than our estimate, 
which we attribute to the impact of shipment timing volatility in higher-ASP AI products. 
We expect sequential revenue growth ahead through Dec 2026, driven by new GPU AI 
server racks ramp-up in 4Q26, which should drive up the ASP , along with strong AI 
spending and expansion of the company’s customer base. The new GPU AI server racks 
have various racks across GPU, CPU, and LPU, supporting ODM growth in coming 
quarters. Sep PC shipment was up 7% MoM but down 32% YoY to 1.5m units, reflecting 
the slow PC demand amid the consumption pull-in under the high memory cost. Despite 
PC shipments growth likely being negative HoH considering the strong pull-in since late 
2025, we expect the strong AI server rack ramp-up to offset the impact, driving 2H26 
revenue up +41% HoH. Maintain Buy with an unchanged TP of NT$291 (12.6x 2027E P/E 
vs. 30.4% NI YoY in 2027-28E).

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Inventec  
We are Neutral-rated on Inventec with a 12-month target price of NT$61.0. Our target 
price is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth 
vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

(2) broadband, IoT, and game console entering peak season. We are positive on

Pegatron’s Sep revenue was up 87% YoY / 143% MoM to NT$215bn, or 64% higher than 
our estimate. This reflects a strong ramp-up in AI servers in Sep. Sep PC shipments were 
up c.8% MoM but down 44% YoY to 0.46m, reflecting the weak PC demand under the 
high memory costs. Prior to September, the company suffered from model transition (i.e. 
declining demand for older models while new models were in a slow shipment ramp-up 
phase).

Earnings revision: We factor in Pegatron’s Sep revenue and raise our 2026-28E net 
income by 4% / 6% / 4%, mainly on higher revenues and lower opex ratio. We raise our 
2026-28E revenues by 16% / 28% / 27%, considering the AI server shipment ramp-up 
and higher ASP on better specification. Given the much lower GM of AI server racks 
compared to Pegatron’s other products, our changes in gross profits narrow to 9% / 12% 
/ 15% in 2026E-28E. The opex ratio for 2026-28E is cut by 0.1 ppt/ 0.2 ppt/ 0.1 ppt to 
reflect the improved operational efficiency.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

VPEC (2455.TW): Optoelectronics mix upgrade driving GM expansion; 
3Q26 in line; Buy
2 November 2026 | 5:30AM HKT
3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

3-month revenue preview: VPEC delivered solid revenue growth at 39% QoQ / 47% 
YoY in 3Q26; Looking ahead, we expect +14% QoQ in 4Q26E, riding on new AI server 
racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade toward 1.6T optical

capture the demand. The company’s Sep revenues sequentially declined by 5% given 
fewer working days, leading to slower procurement from their customers in Japan and 
mainland China. With the revenues delayed to 4Q26, along with strong demand on AI 
data center, we expect monthly revenues to increase sequentially in Oct to Dec, leading 
4Q26 revenues at 14% QoQ.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut our net income by 7% in 2026E, to 
reflect modest revenue moderation from smartphone RF pacing alongside a higher R&D 
expense ratio for AI data center products. Despite the cut, we continue to expect 
sequentially higher revenues in 4Q26E with GM continuing to expand in 4Q26E vs. 3Q26, 
driven by new AI server racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade 
toward 1.6T optical modules, along with capacity expansion and better InP substrate 
supply to better capture the demand. We keep our 2027E-28E estimates largely 
unchanged.

Compared to the Bloomberg consensus, our net income is 9% / 6% higher in 2026E / 
2027E, mainly on higher revenues and GM, reflecting our positive view on its InP 
epiwafers for optical networking across transmitters / CW lasers and receivers, which we 
believe will continue to drive its product mix upgrade, GM expansion, and expand the

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
3481 群創光電(INNOLUX)

報告日期： 2026/10/27 研究員：曾智楷 (Kevin Tseng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收 637 億，毛利率 14.6% ，營利率 2.6% ，EPS 0.57
元。受惠於非顯示器比重持續提升 1H26 EPS 達 0.77 元。  
 公司今年處分三座廠房，累計獲利約為 200 億元，目前資產處
分已告一段落 ， 暫無進一步出售廠房計畫 ， 後續將配合FOPLP
投資調整既有廠區資源。  
 因上半年提前拉貨以及零組件漲價影響，消費型顯示器需求動
能相對保守；半導體事業部分， Chip-first 製程已進入量產，
每月出貨量約 4,000 萬片，而 RDL-first 與 TGV （玻璃基板）
製程部分，目前正與客戶緊密合作推進，預期玻璃基板封裝產
品將於 2028 年推出。

一、 2Q26 毛利率達 14.6% ，EPS 0.57 元 
群創 2Q26 營收 637 億，QoQ -4.6% ，YoY +13.1% ， 而獲利方面，
2Q26 營業毛利 92.7 億， QoQ -3.6% ，YoY +96.8% ，毛利率
14.6% ，QoQ +0.1ppts ，YoY +6.2ppts ，營業淨利 16.3 億，營利
率 2.6% ，2Q26 業外淨利 16.3 億，稅後淨利 46.5 億，單季稅後
EPS 0.57 元。而就上半年來看， 1H26 消費型顯示器佔整體營收
45% ，平均毛利率為 6%至 10% ；商用顯示器佔 13% ，平均毛利
率 16% 至 20%； 非顯示器領域佔42%， 平均毛利率11% 至 15% ，
受惠於商用、非顯示器比重提升， 1H26 EPS 達 0.77 元。

二、資產處分告一段落，後續聚焦 FOPLP 產能  
為充實營運資金、活化公司旗下資產，群創今年處分三座廠房， 3
月將南科模組廠以 8.8 億元售予南茂 (處分利益 6.59 億元 )，以及
將南科二廠以 63.25 億元售予矽品 (獲利約 58 億元 )。公司 4 月再
度宣布，將南科五廠房及附屬設施以總價 148.5 億元售予日月光
投控，預計處分利益高達 133 億元。累計今年群創認列的售廠總
獲利約為 197.59 億元。公司表示，目前資產處分已告一段落，暫
無進一步大規模出售廠房計畫 ， 後續將配合FOPLP 投資調整既有
廠區資源。

三. 消費性面板短期保守，持續朝高階封裝領域布局  
公司公告 9 月營收 199.95 億， MoM +6.9% ，YoY +0.7% ，3Q26
營收 575.8 億， QoQ -9.4% ，YoY -0.4% ，今年累計營收 1879.3
億， YoY +10.6% 。展望後市，在面板業務方面，受記憶體等零組
投資評等：區間操作

全年營收 2659 億元，YoY +8.5% ，毛利率 14.3% ，營利率 3.1% ，
稅後淨利 892 億，全年稅後 EPS 1.12 元，每股淨值 29.51 元。

四. 投資建議為 42.8~54.6 元區間操作  
公司持續投入半導體 FOPLP 領域，聚焦轉型、資產活化題材，但
考量到 4Q26 至明年上半年面板事業展望保守 ， 半導體事業部分，
後續仍須持續觀察客戶認證、量產進度及營運貢獻狀況。目前股
價交易於 2027 BVPS 約 1.64 倍 PBR 水準，以公司過往 PBR 介
於 0.4x~2.5x 區間水準，給予區間操作投資建議，目標價為
42.8~54.6 元(1.45x~1.85 x 2027 BVPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
群創光電 (INNOLUX) 為全球第三大面板廠，主要產品為大尺寸 (大
於 9 吋)與中小尺寸 (9 吋以下 )與延伸技術之薄膜電晶體液晶顯示
器(TFT-LCD) 相關產品，大尺寸產品應用於液晶電視、公共看板、
桌上型監視器、筆記型電腦與工業控制等；中小尺寸則為平板電
腦、可攜式影音播放器、車載、航空船舶、智能家居及手機、穿
戴裝置等應用，也可選擇性搭配各類型觸控面板；另針對特殊使
用需求，也提供應用於醫療、軍用、教育與電子紙應用之產品。
集團總部位於苗栗縣竹南鎮，台灣生產基地位於竹南、南科、台
南、高雄等地，另有海外生產基地則包含中國深圳、中國寧波、
中國南京、中國南海、荷蘭等地。 2025 年公司營收占比大宗消費
品(Commodity) 55% ；非大宗消費品 (Non-commodity) 16% ；非
顯示器領域群 (Non-display) 29% 。公司 NB 客戶為 DELL，液晶電
視客戶包括 Panasonic 、Sharp 、Sony、Toshiba 、Samsung 、TCL
等；手機及平板電腦客戶為 Samsung 、Amazon 、Apple 及華為；
車用面板客戶包括 BMW、福 特、General Motors 、 特斯拉等車廠。
國內主要競爭廠商為友達光電、瀚宇彩晶，國外廠商則包括

2. 群創連售三廠房，預估獲利近 200 億元 
為充實營運資金並挹注未來發展動能，群創近期積極活化旗下資
產。繼 3 月先後將南科模組廠以 8.8 億元售予南茂 (處分利益 6.59
億元 )，以及將南科二廠以 63.25 億元售予矽品 (獲利約 58 億元 )
後，群 創 於15 日再度宣布 ， 將南科五廠房及附屬設施以總價148.5
億元售予日月光投控，預計處分利益高達 133 億元。累計今年以
來，預估公司可望認列售廠總獲利達 197.59 億元。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 需求推動，FOPLP 與玻璃基板市場 2030 年將突破 80 億美元 
根據  Counterpoint Research 報告，受惠於 AI 與高效能運算
(HPC) 強勁需求 ， 全球扇出型面板級封裝(FOPLP) 與玻璃基板市場
規模，預計將從 2024 年的 6.5 億美元飆升至 2030 年的 81 億美
元以上，其中 AI 與 HPC 應用將佔 FOPLP 總營收的 45.6% 。相較
於傳統有機基板，玻璃基板具備高互連密度、尺寸穩定與抗翹曲
等優勢，能支援次世代小晶片 (Chiplet) 架構與大型 AI 處理器，吸
引台積電、 Intel 、三星電機、日月光及力成等大廠積極擴展產能
與研發。區域佈局上，預計 2030 年台灣、日本與中國大陸將囊括
全球 84.8% 的面板級封裝產能。儘管目前仍面臨面板尺寸標準化
與玻璃穿孔 (TGV) 製程等量產挑戰，但在產業鏈合作推動下，整體
市場前景依舊十分樂觀。

圖三、 2024-2030 年各地區 FOPLP 面板產能預估

資料來源： Counterpoint

2. 零組件成本壓力與需求衰退交織，10 月面板價格全面持平 
根據 TrendForce 預估，進入第 4 季後，受上游零組件成本上漲壓
力遽增及終端採購需求衰退雙重影響， 10 月 TV、MNT 與 NB 三
大面板價格預估將全面持平。 TV 面板部分，雖有原廠調控產能並
喊漲，但主要品牌第 4 季採購需求季減約 5%，短期難接受漲價；
而 MNT 面板需求持續減弱，惟成本壓力使面板廠無讓步空間，買
賣雙方對持平具共識，後續將視 TV 面板能否成功漲價而定；另
外， NB 面板則因前六大品牌第 4 季採購需求恐季減近 30% ，出
貨動能嚴重萎縮，在成本高漲且降價無助爭取訂單的情況下，雙
方亦達成維持價格穩定的共識。

Document 195: 9月规模以上工业利润数据解读：9月规模以上工业利润总额同比明显提高

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月规模以上工业利润数据解读：9月规模以上工业利润总额同比明显提高.pdf
Report source: 国新证券股份
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年10月27日
事件
10 月 27 日，国家统计局发布，1-9 月，全国规模以上工业企
业实现利润总额 60337.4 亿元，同比增长 15.5%（按可比口径计
算）。
解读
一、1-9 月规上工业利润同比保持较高增长，9 月增速比 8 月
明显提高
1-9月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额 60337.4 亿元，
同比增长 15.5%，比 1-8 月回落 0.2 个百分点，比 2025 年同期提
高 12.3个百分点，创 2022年以来同期同比增速新高。
其中，9月，规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长 14.4%，比
8月提高 10.2个百分点，比 2025年 9月回落 7.2个百分点。
1-9 月，采矿业利润总额同比增长 38.2%，比 1-8 月提高 3.1
个百分点，制造业利润总额同比增长 17.0%，比 1-8月回落 0.4个
百分点，电力热力水的生产供应业同比-14.3%，降幅比 1-8月扩大
2.3个百分点。
9月，规模以上工业利润总额同比增速明显提高，主要受 PPI

上涨的影响。9月 PPI环比上涨 1.2%，比 8月提高 0.8个百分点，
同比上涨 5.0%，涨幅比 8 月提高 1.2 个百分点，环比同比涨幅均
扩大，当月利润改善明显。
二、1-9 月股份制企业利润较高增长，行业利润增速极端分化
1-9月，国有控股规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长 13.1%，
比 1-8月提高 2.8个百分点；股份制企业利润总额同比增长 20.8%，
比 1-8月提高 0.4百分点；外商及港澳台商投资企业利润总额同比
增长 0.5%，比 1-8 月回落 1.8 个百分点；私营企业利润总额增长
8.7%，比 1-8月回落 1.7个百分点。
1-9 月，在 39 个可比工业大类行业中，17 个行业利润同比增
长，22个行业利润总额同比下降。
在利润总额超千亿的行业中，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制
造业利润同比增长 103.8%，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业利润同比
增长 79.7%，有色金属矿采选业利润总额同比增长 68.0%，化学原
宏观研究
9月规模以上工业利润总额同比明显提高

——9月规模以上工业利润数据解读
市场表现截至 2026.9.30
资料来源：国家统计局,国新证券整理。
分析师：郝大明
登记编码：S1490514010002
邮箱：haodaming@crsec.com.cn
证券研究报告

### Page 2

料和化学制品制造业利润同比增长 49.2%，煤炭开采和洗选业利润
同比增长 57.0%，石油和天然气开采业利润同比增长 21.3%。
1-9月，汽车制造业、酒饮料和精制茶制造业、电气机械和器
材制造业、电力热力生产和供应业利润总额同比分别-22.8%、-
14.3%、-5.9%、-17.9%。
三、重点产业链利润增速较高
电子相关行业利润高速增长。1-9 月，电子行业利润同比增长
1.0倍，比 1-8月回落 10个百分点。其中，电子专用材料制造、半
导体分立器件制造行业利润分别增长 2.2倍、51.7%；新一代信息
技术加速发展，带动光电子器件制造、电子电路制造行业利润分别
增长 69.8%、62.5%。
高技术制造业引领作用明显。1-9 月，规模以上高技术制造业
利润同比增长 52.0%，比 1-8 月回落 2.7个百分点。其中，光纤制
造、光缆制造行业利润分别增长 5.7倍和 1.1 倍；计算机外围设备
制造、计算机整机制造、工业控制计算机及系统制造行业利润分别
增长 2.8倍、1.7倍、90.9%。

原材料制造业利润快速增长。1-9 月，规模以上原材料制造业
利润同比增长 44.9%，比 1-8 月回落 2.4个百分点。其中，化学原
料和化学制品制造业利润增长 49.2%，比 1-8月回落 1.8个百分点；
有色金属冶炼和压延加工业利润同比增长 79.7%，比 1-8 月回落
3.2 个百分点；石油加工行业同比扭亏为盈，实现利润总额 842.2
亿元，比 1-8月增加 279.3亿元。
总体看，1-9月原材料制造业、高技术制造业、电子行业等行
业利润大幅增长，规模以上工业企业利润较高增长。9月 PPI环比、
同比涨幅扩大，工业利润增速明显提高。预测四季度工业利润同比
增速将比三季度小幅提高。
投资建议
1：1-9月规上原材料制造业利润总额同比较高增长。
2：1-9月规上新高技术制造业利润高增长。
3：1-9月规上电子相关行业利润高增长。
风险提示
1、地缘政治恶化超预期；2、欧美经济衰退超预期。

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

Appendix

Document 242: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

2 BloombergNEF , AI Buildout Drives 76% Power Bill Jump on Largest US Grid, May 14, 2026.

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

in portfolios.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Document 300: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause , however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downsid e 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would  remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widel y 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is  
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals” . By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutini sed. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA 1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike.  But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, t he decision is justified by the need to further analy se risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that an y rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United St ates). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the H ICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1% -2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged
